qualified_current_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-023

PROPERTY_NAME

Employment security, trust and fair distribution of productivity gains

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Toyota's post-1950 labour compact and later joint declarations; NUMMI employment-security practice.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT_PROPERTY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Link productivity improvement with stable employment, working conditions and labour-management trust.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Workers rationally conceal improvement opportunities when improvement threatens jobs, workload or bargaining position.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Trust destruction, sabotage, hidden problems, fear-based compliance and 'lean and mean' layoffs.

MECHANISM

Set credible no-layoff or redeployment rules for improvement gains where feasible; negotiate workload; share gains; protect reporting and transition.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Improvement solicits worker knowledge, reduces labour content or changes job design materially.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For small changes with no employment/workload effect, state that boundary and monitor cumulative effects.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Credible management commitment; financial capacity; worker representation/voice; redeployment and training; transparent gain accounting.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

More truthful problem exposure, cooperation, capability retention and lower fear.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Promises withdrawn in downturns; gains captured by owners; redeployment becomes intensification; insiders protected while suppliers/contractors absorb risk.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Toyota's 1950 dispute and wider Lean layoffs show security is not inherent; national institutions and firm economics matter.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Historical employment and labor agreements motivate this reconstruction's explicit fair-transition questions. Their necessity and effectiveness vary; neither a universal no-layoff rule nor a causal guarantee is established.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Before labour-saving improvement, define employment, workload, compensation, redeployment and supplier effects; treat trust as evidence-bearing governance.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Respect-for-people slogans, town halls and culture statements do not establish security or fair gains.

EL-024

EL-024

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-024

PROPERTY_NAME

Management responsibility for system conditions and escalation response

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Toyota supervisory/management system, TQC and later TPS learning-system interpretations.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Managers create conditions, respond to abnormalities and support workers in solving problems.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Local teams are held accountable for causes created by staffing, incentives, architecture, contracts or policy.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Blame shifting, repeated local fixes, ignored escalation and transformation decay.

MECHANISM

Assign accountable system owners; require timely response; remove cross-boundary impediments; fund capability/buffers; review whether incentives create the failure.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

A problem exceeds local authority, recurs across teams or reflects structural operating conditions.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For a bounded local issue, delegate action with a clear escalation threshold.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Authority over resources and policy; transparent response expectations; independent review; outcome and labour measures.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Structural causes addressed, credible stop/voice systems and sustained improvement.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Leader gemba theatre; delegation without resources; hero intervention; changing priorities; local blame; executive metrics conflict with doctrine.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Proponent accounts often make management commitment an unfalsifiable explanation for success or failure.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Shifted from supervisor response to explicit system ownership, governance and accountability for incentives and preconditions.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Leaders own the conditions they control, respond to escalations, arbitrate cross-system trade-offs and are measured on outcomes, resilience and worker effects.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Leader standard work, gemba walks and coaching kata are optional; accountable system condition ownership is the property.

CURRENT_STATUS

STRONGLY_RETAINED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

MODERATE_CONVERGENT

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S005

  2. S010

  3. S013

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S024

  2. S029

  3. S030

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S027

  2. S030

  3. S044

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How can management responsibility be measured without turning 'leadership commitment' into a post hoc success explanation?

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-022

  2. EL-025

  3. EL-038

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Are repeated local failures traceable to conditions only management or a system owner can change?

  2. Does escalation produce a bounded response and decision?

  3. Are leaders accountable for labour, resilience and incentive side effects, not only throughput?

EL-025

EL-025

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-025

PROPERTY_NAME

Whole-system optimisation and proxy/metric governance

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

TPS anti-overproduction and system flow; Lean 'optimise the whole'; modern operations and software measurement.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

HYBRID

ORIGINAL_FORM

Subordinate local production to the needs of the downstream process and whole value stream.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Teams game local throughput, cycle time, defect, utilisation or productivity metrics while system outcomes deteriorate.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Local optimisation, Goodhart effects, hidden queues, quality sacrifice and worker harm.

MECHANISM

Use a balanced causal measurement set; define system boundary and countermetrics; avoid individual competition; audit gaming and revise definitions.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Metrics affect incentives or decisions across interacting components.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For a small team with direct outcome visibility, use a few narrative/quantitative checks rather than a dashboard programme.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Outcome model; stable metric definitions; cross-boundary data; qualitative challenge; no punishment for adverse signals.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Better decisions, fewer proxy pathologies and alignment of flow, quality, resilience, value and wellbeing.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Dashboard proliferation; composite-score opacity; denominator manipulation; item splitting; suppressed incidents; cross-context ranking; measurement burden.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Lean measures are frequently used for labour control; DORA and SPACE explicitly warn against single metrics and inappropriate comparisons.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From local flow and waste measures to multidimensional system indicators, countermetrics and explicit anti-gaming governance.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Treat metrics as revisable evidence, not targets or truth; pair throughput with quality, outcome, resilience and human effects.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Obeya boards, SQDC panels, burndowns or DORA dashboards are optional; decision-valid measurement is the property.

CURRENT_STATUS

USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_CONVERGENT

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S005

  2. S018

  3. S053

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S025

  2. S026

  3. S065

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S058

  2. S059

  3. S062

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How can metric gaming be detected before it materially distorts the work?

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-001

  2. EL-003

  3. EL-009

  4. EL-023

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Do metrics represent the whole outcome or create a local target that can be gamed?

  2. Are quality, resilience, wellbeing and hidden queues countermeasured?

  3. Can definitions and incentives be revised when behaviour adapts to the metric?

EL-026
id

EL-026

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-026

PROPERTY_NAME

Equipment, service and delivery-system reliability and maintainability

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

TPS equipment availability and preventive maintenance; TPM quality ancestry; modern reliability engineering.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Maintain equipment capability so low-WIP flow is not repeatedly interrupted.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Tight flow exposes breakdowns, but exposure without reliability capability creates service failure.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Unplanned downtime, repeated emergency repair, unsafe degradation and buffer depletion.

MECHANISM

Identify critical assets/services; monitor failure and degradation; perform risk-based preventive/predictive maintenance; design for diagnosis, repair and recovery.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Failure interrupts flow materially, has safety/quality consequences or consumes strategic buffers.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For noncritical, cheap and rapidly replaceable components, use run-to-failure with spares and a tested replacement path.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Criticality model; maintenance windows and capacity; skilled responders; spares; configuration history; feedback from incidents.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Higher availability, lower variability, safer operation and smaller required buffers.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Maintenance theatre; over-maintenance; bad predictive models; deferred maintenance to hit throughput; hidden technical debt; single-expert dependence.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Low inventory can convert ordinary failures into crises, while TPM programmes can become checklist bundles without reliability economics.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From factory preventive maintenance to service reliability, observability, error budgets, graceful degradation and recovery engineering.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Choose maintenance and recovery policy by criticality, failure distribution, consequence and replacement economics.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

TPM pillars, maintenance boards and autonomous-maintenance checklists are optional implementations.

CURRENT_STATUS

STRONGLY_RETAINED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_CONVERGENT

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S005

  2. S004

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S032

  2. S039

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S041

  2. S042

  3. S057

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should maintenance policy incorporate correlated software, cyber and supplier failures with sparse history?

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

Reliability reduces variability and buffer demand. Over-maintenance adds cost; under-maintenance causes disruption. Cheap path is tested run-to-failure for noncritical assets.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-006

  2. EL-008

  3. EL-009

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Does the target identify reliability-critical equipment, services and dependencies?

  2. Is maintenance chosen from failure and recovery economics rather than ritual frequency?

  3. Would reducing buffers expose an unaddressed reliability defect?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

qualified_current_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-026

PROPERTY_NAME

Equipment, service and delivery-system reliability and maintainability

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

TPS equipment availability and preventive maintenance; TPM quality ancestry; modern reliability engineering.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY

ORIGINAL_FORM

Maintain equipment capability so low-WIP flow is not repeatedly interrupted.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Tight flow exposes breakdowns, but exposure without reliability capability creates service failure.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Unplanned downtime, repeated emergency repair, unsafe degradation and buffer depletion.

MECHANISM

Identify critical assets/services; monitor failure and degradation; perform risk-based preventive/predictive maintenance; design for diagnosis, repair and recovery.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Failure interrupts flow materially, has safety/quality consequences or consumes strategic buffers.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For noncritical, cheap and rapidly replaceable components, use run-to-failure with spares and a tested replacement path.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Criticality model; maintenance windows and capacity; skilled responders; spares; configuration history; feedback from incidents.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Higher availability, lower variability, safer operation and smaller required buffers.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Maintenance theatre; over-maintenance; bad predictive models; deferred maintenance to hit throughput; hidden technical debt; single-expert dependence.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Low inventory can convert ordinary failures into crises, while TPM programmes can become checklist bundles without reliability economics.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Factory maintenance is historically connected to TPS. Service observability, error budgets and recovery engineering are contemporary convergences/analogues unless a specific transmission is established.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Choose maintenance and recovery policy by criticality, failure distribution, consequence and replacement economics.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

TPM pillars, maintenance boards and autonomous-maintenance checklists are optional implementations.

CURRENT_STATUS

STRONGLY_RETAINED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_CONVERGENT

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S005

  2. S004

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S032

  2. S039

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S041

  2. S042

  3. S057

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should maintenance policy incorporate correlated software, cyber and supplier failures with sparse history?

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

Reliability reduces variability and buffer demand. Over-maintenance adds cost; under-maintenance causes disruption. Cheap path is tested run-to-failure for noncritical assets.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-006

  2. EL-008

  3. EL-009

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Does the target identify reliability-critical equipment, services and dependencies?

  2. Is maintenance chosen from failure and recovery economics rather than ritual frequency?

  3. Would reducing buffers expose an unaddressed reliability defect?

EL-027
id

EL-027

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-027

PROPERTY_NAME

Supplier and dependency capability development with fair risk allocation

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Toyota supplier associations, quality/TPS training and interfirm learning network.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Develop suppliers' quality, delivery and improvement capability rather than inspect or bargain only on price.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

End-to-end performance is limited by external capability and incentives outside the focal organisation.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Recurring supplier defects, unstable replenishment, adversarial cost transfer and weak joint learning.

MECHANISM

Share requirements and knowledge; jointly solve capability gaps; use fair contracts, gain/risk sharing and escalation; measure supplier health as well as price.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

A dependency is recurrent, strategically material and improvable through relationship-specific learning.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For commodity, low-risk purchases, use clear specifications, diversified sources and routine qualification rather than a development programme.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Long-term relationship; technical openness; bargaining fairness; supplier capacity; protection of proprietary knowledge; exit alternatives.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Higher quality, faster learning, less disruption and lower total cost.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Coercive price reduction; unpaid improvement; lock-in; focal-firm appropriation of gains; supplier overinvestment; lower-tier labour harm.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Toyota partnership narratives can obscure power asymmetry; synchronisation may transfer buffers, capital and risk to suppliers.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Mature form adds supplier viability, fair gain/risk allocation, labour standards, lower-tier visibility and recovery planning.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Develop critical dependency capability only where joint value exceeds coordination and lock-in cost, with explicit fairness and resilience safeguards.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Supplier kaizen workshops, scorecards or associations are optional; joint capability and fair allocation are the property.

CURRENT_STATUS

SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT_PROPERTY

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

MODERATE_EMPIRICAL_MIXED_DISTRIBUTIONAL

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S005

  2. S010

  3. S069

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S039

  2. S040

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S038

  2. S041

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Independent evidence on how Toyota-style supplier development distributes gains, workload and risk across tiers remains incomplete.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

CROSS_FUNCTIONAL

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-008

  2. EL-023

  3. EL-028

  4. EL-039

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Is a critical supplier being developed or merely pressured to absorb cost and inventory?

  2. Are gains, buffers, workload and disruption risk allocated transparently and fairly?

  3. Would diversification be cheaper and safer than deep relationship-specific development?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

EL-028

EL-028

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-028

PROPERTY_NAME

Multi-tier dependency visibility and resilience segmentation

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Post-2011 Toyota supply-chain mapping and broader resilience research.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

REACTION_TO_LEAN

ORIGINAL_FORM

After disruption, identify lower-tier sites, affected items, inventories and restoration alternatives.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Tier-one visibility hides common sub-tier dependencies, concentration and long recovery paths.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Correlated disruption, unknown single points of failure, slow recovery and false diversification.

MECHANISM

Map critical item/site dependencies beyond the first tier; classify by lead time, substitutability and consequence; prepare restoration, alternate design/source and data-maintenance routines.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

A dependency can stop a material outcome, has long recovery, opaque tiers or geographic/technical concentration.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For low-criticality, rapidly substitutable inputs, record vendor/category and test replacement rather than map the whole network.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Supplier cooperation; data governance; item-site identity; criticality model; alternative qualification; regular updates.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Earlier disruption recognition, targeted buffers and faster alternate sourcing or redesign.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Huge stale maps; supplier confidentiality conflict; false sense of control; concentration persists despite visibility; mapping cost exceeds value.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Mapping does not itself create resilience and can shift reporting burden to small suppliers.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From JIT synchronisation to segmented JIT/JIC, virtual dualisation, critical-stock and recoverability design.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Maintain decision-useful deep-tier visibility only for critical dependencies, coupled to buffer, alternate and recovery action.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A proprietary supply-chain map or Toyota RESCUE system is not required; a critical-dependency register may suffice.

CURRENT_STATUS

STRONGLY_RETAINED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_POST_DISRUPTION_DOMAIN

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S035

  2. S036

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S039

  2. S040

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S037

  2. S040

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Methods for validating supplier-reported lower-tier data and common-cause dependence remain immature.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

Resilience profile: visibility does not reduce flow by itself; it enables selective buffers, alternates and recovery. Overmapping wastes effort; undermapping hides correlated failure.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-027

  2. EL-034

  3. EL-039

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Does the system know which lower-tier dependencies can stop the outcome and how long recovery takes?

  2. Is visibility linked to an alternate, buffer, redesign or restoration decision?

  3. Can low-criticality dependencies follow a cheap path instead of exhaustive mapping?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

qualified_current_record

EL-029
id

EL-029

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-029

PROPERTY_NAME

Set-based option preservation and delayed irreversible convergence

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Toyota product-development research and set-based concurrent engineering.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

LEAN_PRODUCT_DEVELOPMENT_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Explore ranges of alternatives, communicate constraints and narrow sets as feasibility evidence accumulates.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Early point decisions create lock-in, late redesign and fragile optimisation under uncertainty.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Premature convergence, expensive reversal and loss of feasible alternatives.

MECHANISM

Map design space and constraints; keep multiple feasible options; run discriminating tests; eliminate options with evidence; commit at the last responsible point.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Uncertainty, coupling and irreversibility are high enough that a wrong early choice is costly and parallel learning is affordable.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

When the decision is reversible, familiar or low-coupling, choose one option and preserve a fallback note.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Capacity for parallel analysis; explicit decision criteria; integration/trade-off knowledge; convergence deadlines; ownership.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Reduced late rework, better trade-offs and more robust commitment.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Unbounded option proliferation; delayed decisions; duplicated work; superficial sets; hidden resource cost; no convergence rule.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Industrial implementation maturity and causal performance evidence remain limited; not every domain benefits.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Separated from manufacturing waste elimination and reframed as an uncertainty/option-value mechanism.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Preserve only decision-relevant alternatives until evidence changes expected regret; record eliminated options and convergence rationale.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Trade-off curves, set matrices and obeya rooms are optional; evidence-calibrated option preservation is the property.

CURRENT_STATUS

CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

MODERATE_CASE_BASED

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S049

  2. S050

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S051

  2. S052

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S049

  2. S052

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Strong comparative causal evidence outside automotive/product design remains limited.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

CROSS_FUNCTIONAL

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

Exploration intentionally creates parallel WIP; favour it where option value exceeds queue and coordination cost. Conflicts with premature flow optimisation.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-004

  2. EL-005

  3. EL-007

  4. EL-036

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Is uncertainty and irreversibility high enough to justify preserving alternatives?

  2. What evidence eliminates an option, and when must convergence occur?

  3. Would parallel exploration reduce expected rework or merely duplicate effort?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

qualified_current_record

EL-030
id

EL-030

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-030

PROPERTY_NAME

Cross-functional product-development integration and reusable trade-off knowledge

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Comparative automotive product-development research and Toyota LPD interpretations.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

LEAN_PRODUCT_DEVELOPMENT_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Integrate product, process and organisational knowledge under strong project leadership; reuse constraints and trade-offs.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Functional silos discover incompatibility late and repeatedly relearn design constraints.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Late engineering changes, handoff loss, manufacturing/service incompatibility and repeated analysis.

MECHANISM

Create cross-functional decision forums; maintain constraint interfaces, trade-off evidence and reusable knowledge; give project leadership authority to resolve conflicts.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Product outcomes couple multiple disciplines, lifecycle stages or downstream operations.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For a small single-discipline change, direct peer review and a concise decision record suffice.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Representative disciplines; decision authority; shared models; knowledge stewardship; incentives for lifecycle rather than functional optimisation.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Faster convergence, fewer late changes and compounding design knowledge.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Meeting overload; heavyweight bureaucracy; knowledge repositories without use; dominant function suppresses dissent; outdated trade-off curves.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Automotive evidence may not transfer; LPD lacks agreed definition and complete coherent implementation evidence.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From heavyweight project management toward digital thread, model-based trade-offs, concurrent assurance and learning reuse.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Integrate affected disciplines at consequential decisions and preserve reusable evidence at interfaces—not every participant in every meeting.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Obeya rooms, chief-engineer titles and trade-off curves are optional implementations.

CURRENT_STATUS

RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

MODERATE_COMPARATIVE_AUTOMOTIVE

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S049

  2. S050

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S051

  2. S052

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S049

  2. S052

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Which integration structures work best outside automotive and how reusable knowledge remains valid under technology change are unresolved.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

CROSS_FUNCTIONAL

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-002

  2. EL-020

  3. EL-029

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Are coupled disciplines represented before irreversible decisions?

  2. Is trade-off and constraint evidence reusable and freshness-controlled?

  3. Would an integration mechanism resolve actual late conflict or only create more meetings?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

EL-031
id

EL-031

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-031

PROPERTY_NAME

Software delivery flow with automation, observability, reliability and recovery

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Lean software/Kanban translations combined with Continuous Delivery, DevOps and reliability engineering.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

HYBRID

ORIGINAL_FORM

Translate flow, small batches, quality-at-source and WIP limitation into software work.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Software changes wait in queues, integrate late, fail opaquely or reach production without safe recovery.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Large risky releases, slow feedback, hidden operational defects and speed-at-the-expense-of-stability.

MECHANISM

Limit active work; integrate frequently; automate build/test/deploy; observe production; use progressive delivery, rollback and incident learning; validate user outcomes.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Software changes are frequent enough that manual release and delayed integration impose risk or delay.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For infrequent low-risk software, use reproducible builds, basic automated tests and a documented release/recovery path.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Version control; trustworthy tests; environment/configuration control; observability; on-call/recovery capability; product outcome feedback.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Faster and safer change, shorter learning cycles and reduced release blast radius.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Pipeline theatre; flaky tests; metric gaming; developer burnout; rapid delivery of low-value work; fragile platforms; operations burden hidden.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Software evidence is largely observational and case-based; factory metaphors miss discovery, architecture and invisible cognitive work.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Kanban's flow ideas were hybridised with software-native automation, reliability, security and observability.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Optimise sustainable software change across throughput and instability, with small reversible changes, outcome validation and humane operations.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A kanban board, daily deployment or DORA dashboard is not required; reliable, observable and reversible delivery capability is the property.

CURRENT_STATUS

RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

MODERATE_LARGE_OBSERVATIONAL

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S053

  2. S054

  3. S057

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S055

  2. S056

  3. S059

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S055

  2. S056

  3. S058

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Causal effect sizes, optimal limits and metric robustness across software domains remain unresolved.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

CROSS_FUNCTIONAL

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

Value: shorter software feedback and smaller failure scope. Assumes automation quality, operability and recovery. Overuse creates metric gaming, toil and acceleration without value.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-001

  2. EL-004

  3. EL-023

  4. EL-025

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Can the system deliver small changes safely, observe effects and recover without heroics?

  2. Are delivery metrics used as system signals rather than individual targets?

  3. Does faster delivery improve user/system outcomes or merely increase change volume?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

EL-032

EL-032

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-032

PROPERTY_NAME

Portfolio selection, stopping and retirement of low-value work

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Lean value/waste generalisation, product-development learning and modern product/flow management.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

Eliminate work that does not create value and stop overproduction.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Organisations optimise execution while accepting too much low-value work and retaining obsolete products, controls or commitments.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Portfolio congestion, zombie work, sunk-cost continuation and capacity starvation.

MECHANISM

Set admission, continuation and retirement criteria; compare expected value and learning against total cost; stop, defer or archive work explicitly.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Demand exceeds capacity, work ages without evidence, or a value hypothesis fails.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

For a tiny backlog, review informally at a defined interval and close stale items.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Outcome evidence; total-cost visibility; authority to stop; reversible archival/transition; stakeholder communication.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Freed capacity, lower WIP, less maintenance burden and focus on consequential outcomes.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Politically convenient cancellation; short-termism; abandoned users; hidden transition costs; gaming by redefining work as mandatory.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Waste language can erase care, resilience or exploratory work whose value is indirect or delayed.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From eliminating production waste to governing portfolio entry, continuation, deimplementation and retirement.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Stop or retire work only through an evidence-bearing decision that accounts for beneficiary, option value, transition and external costs.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Backlog purges, stop-start-continue workshops and portfolio kanban are optional.

CURRENT_STATUS

CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

MODERATE_CONVERGENT

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S018

  2. S053

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S060

  2. S065

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S044

  2. S058

  3. S062

OPEN_QUESTIONS

How should long-horizon public value and option value be preserved when near-term evidence is weak?

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-001

  2. EL-008

  3. EL-029

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Can low-value or stale work be stopped, deferred and retired rather than merely completed faster?

  2. Does the stop decision account for transition, users, resilience and option value?

  3. Is a lightweight periodic review sufficient for this portfolio?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

EL-033
id

EL-033

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-033

PROPERTY_NAME

Universal waste elimination as the master objective

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Later Lean simplification of Ohno's waste categories and Lean Thinking.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Identify and eliminate all non-value-adding activity.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Real waste can consume capacity, obscure defects and delay value.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None as a universal master objective; the maxim itself can create harm.

MECHANISM

As historically deployed, classify activity as waste and remove it.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. Specific waste removal must be justified through EL-001, EL-002, EL-008 and outcome evidence.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Name the concrete failure mode and remove only the activity that causes it; do not launch generic waste hunts.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Valid value model, full system boundary, labour/resilience analysis and evidence that the activity is avoidable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Can remove genuine delay, rework or unnecessary effort when narrowly applied.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Removes resilience, learning, care, maintenance, worker discretion or necessary redundancy; becomes headcount reduction.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Waste is easier to name than value; universal elimination ignores variability, option value and externalities.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Superseded by explicit outcome, queue, buffer, reliability and portfolio mechanisms.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

No independent property. Ask which failure mode the alleged waste causes and what protective function it may serve.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Waste walks, seven/eight-waste posters and red-tag events are not evidence.

CURRENT_STATUS

SUPERSEDED_BY_STRONGER_FORM

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_CRITICAL_CONVERGENT

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S007

  2. S018

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S024

  2. S025

  3. S065

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S032

  2. S033

  3. S060

OPEN_QUESTIONS

The boundary between avoidable burden and protective slack remains context-specific, not a universal taxonomy.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-001

  2. EL-008

  3. EL-023

  4. EL-029

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. What concrete failure mode does the alleged waste cause?

  2. Does the activity provide resilience, learning, safety, care or option value?

  3. Would removing it improve the whole outcome or only a local cost metric?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

Rejected as an independent crosswalk property; retained only as a prompt subordinate to stronger properties.

EL-033

bearer

The universal rule to eliminate every item labelled waste

scientific_origin

Rejected universal; replaced by explicit conditional mechanisms

incremental_control_cost

Overcorrection can remove useful reserves or turn analysis into new ceremony.

qualitative_evaluation_criterion

Ask what protective or learning function is lost before removal; no claim that all waste terminology is useless.

EL-034
id

EL-034

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-034

PROPERTY_NAME

Minimum or zero inventory everywhere

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Toolkit caricature of JIT and low-stock Lean programmes.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Drive inventory toward zero to expose problems and release capital.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Excess stock can hide instability, delay feedback and consume capital.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None at universal scope; the maxim creates unbuffered risk.

MECHANISM

Remove inventory and rely on replenishment or expediting.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. Inventory reduction is justified only after component-specific buffer economics.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Measure criticality, lead time, obsolescence and recovery; remove only demonstrably excess stock.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Reliable replenishment; alternate capacity; bounded demand/lead-time variability; recovery capability.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Lower carrying cost and faster defect feedback within a safe operating region.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Stockouts, production/service interruption, supplier pressure, emergency freight and correlated disruption.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Toyota sources specify minimum necessary stock, not zero; empirical performance is often concave and disruption research rejects binary JIT/JIC.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Replaced by EL-008 and EL-028: segmented buffer portfolios and dependency resilience.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

NO GENERAL PROPERTY; choose inventory by total expected cost and resilience.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Kanban quantity cuts and inventory-reduction targets are not inherently beneficial.

CURRENT_STATUS

REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S002

  2. S006

  3. S067

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S033

  2. S039

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S032

  2. S035

  3. S040

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Optimal stock under deep uncertainty and correlated shocks remains difficult, but that uncertainty strengthens rather than weakens the rejection of zero inventory.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

Over-removal shifts variability to capacity/time and creates starvation. Mature substitute: EL-008.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-004

  2. EL-008

  3. EL-028

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Is inventory demonstrably excess after accounting for lead time, consequence and recovery?

  2. Which buffer will absorb variability after inventory is removed?

  3. Could the same benefit come from reliability or setup improvement without reducing protection?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

Rejected universal rule; component-specific inventory optimisation remains valid.

EL-035
id

EL-035

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-035

PROPERTY_NAME

Maximum utilisation and zero slack

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Mass-production efficiency logic and cost-cutting Lean appropriations, not a defensible TPS invariant.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Keep labour and equipment continuously busy and remove idle capacity.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Visible idle resources can be costly, but under variability utilisation is not throughput.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None at universal scope.

MECHANISM

Increase load, cut staffing and fill all available capacity.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. High planned utilisation may be acceptable only with low variability and cheap delay.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Measure queues and service risk; release spare capacity only where it has no response or resilience function.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Near-deterministic arrivals/service, low failure consequence and external buffers.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

May lower unit cost in a narrow stable operating region.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Nonlinear queues, burnout, no improvement/response time, brittle recovery and hidden overtime.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Operations science shows capacity is one of the required variability buffers; labour evidence links intensification to harm.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Rejected and replaced by queue-aware capacity plus explicit strategic slack.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Target sustainable service and total cost, not maximum busy time.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Utilisation dashboards, staffing-to-takt formulas and efficiency targets do not establish value.

CURRENT_STATUS

REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S005

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S025

  2. S026

  3. S070

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S032

  2. S027

  3. S028

OPEN_QUESTIONS

The socially acceptable and economically optimal reserve varies by service obligation and risk.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

High utilisation under variability creates queues; substitute EL-008/EL-009.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-008

  2. EL-009

  3. EL-023

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Is spare capacity an unneeded cost or the system's response, learning and recovery buffer?

  2. What queue and wellbeing effects follow the utilisation target?

  3. Could demand shaping or reliability improvement close the cost gap instead?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

Rejected universal rule.

EL-036
id

EL-036

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-036

PROPERTY_NAME

One-piece flow or the smallest possible batch everywhere

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Factory ideal generalised beyond its economic conditions.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Move one item continuously through adjacent processes.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Large batches can create delay and rework.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None as a universal minimum-batch rule.

MECHANISM

Force work into single-item or arbitrarily tiny units.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. Use only where setup/coordination is low, routing is repeatable and one-unit movement improves economics.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Choose the smallest economically coherent and safely verifiable batch, not literal one-piece flow.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Low setup and transport cost; balanced reliable process; modular output; compatible validation.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Excellent feedback and low WIP in suitable repetitive flows.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Fragmentation, excessive handoffs, unsafe partial output, validation overhead, loss of system coherence and starvation.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Knowledge/product development may benefit from coherent experiments or parallel option sets; HMLV settings require adaptation.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Narrowed into EL-005 batch economics and EL-029 option-value reasoning.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

NO GENERAL PROPERTY; choose reversible economic batches by context.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

U-shaped cells, single-card movement and one-story-at-a-time rules are optional local designs.

CURRENT_STATUS

REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_MECHANISTIC_CONTEXTUAL

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S004

  2. S007

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S032

  2. S052

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S057

  2. S063

  3. S064

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Batch economics under complex integration and shared validation remain an engineering-analysis problem.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

TEAM

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

Small batches shorten feedback until setup/coordination/validation dominates. Substitute EL-005.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-005

  2. EL-029

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. What is the economic and epistemic batch, including setup and validation?

  2. Would a smaller unit remain coherent and safe?

  3. Does literal one-piece flow solve a measured queue or import a factory ideal?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

Rejected universal rule; local one-piece flow can still be optimal.

qualified_current_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-036

PROPERTY_NAME

One-piece flow or the smallest possible batch everywhere

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Factory ideal generalised beyond its economic conditions.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Move one item continuously through adjacent processes.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Large batches can create delay and rework.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None as a universal minimum-batch rule.

MECHANISM

Force work into single-item or arbitrarily tiny units.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. Use only where setup/coordination is low, routing is repeatable and one-unit movement improves economics.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Choose the smallest economically coherent and safely verifiable batch, not literal one-piece flow.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Low setup and transport cost; balanced reliable process; modular output; compatible validation.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Excellent feedback and low WIP in suitable repetitive flows.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Fragmentation, excessive handoffs, unsafe partial output, validation overhead, loss of system coherence and starvation.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Knowledge/product development may benefit from coherent experiments or parallel option sets; HMLV settings require adaptation.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Narrowed into EL-005 batch economics and EL-029 option-value reasoning.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

NO GENERAL PROPERTY; choose reversible economic batches by context.

property_id

EL-036

bearer

The universal instruction to use the smallest possible batch

scientific_origin

Rejected universal with small-batch positive control

current_evidence_warrant

Setup costs can outweigh reduced delay; lowering setup can reverse the preferred batch. This is an economic counterexample, not a field effect estimate.

incremental_control_cost

Setup, validation and coherence costs versus feedback delay.

qualitative_evaluation_criterion

Compare feasible batches and costs; retain single-piece flow when the relevant setup and risks permit it.

EL-037
id

EL-037

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-037

PROPERTY_NAME

Exact Toyota-tool or named-method conformance

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Global Lean diffusion through visible tools, consulting programmes and certification.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Install kanban, 5S, A3, VSM, standard-work sheets, andon and kaizen events as a Lean system.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Tools can embody useful mechanisms, but conformance is often mistaken for capability.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None as an independent property.

MECHANISM

Audit presence, format and usage of named practices.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. A named practice should be chosen only if it is the cheapest effective implementation of a retained property.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Implement the property in the target system's native representation; document why no Lean artefact is needed.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Mechanism match, user fit, maintenance, decision impact and evidence.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Can accelerate communication where users share the practice and the form fits the work.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Cargo cult, duplicate tooling, audit theatre, resistance, stale artefacts and false maturity.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Empirical implementation research finds soft/system conditions distinguish outcomes more than possession of hard tools.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Rejected as methodology conformance; ceremony stripping maps every practice to mechanism and alternatives.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

NO GENERAL PROPERTY. Evaluate mechanism, trigger, burden, alternatives and outcome.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

This candidate is ceremony itself.

CURRENT_STATUS

CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_CONVERGENT

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S018

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S022

  2. S024

  3. S030

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S030

  2. S055

  3. S066

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Some forms may offer standardisation or communication benefits, but those must be evaluated per context.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-003

  2. EL-017

  3. EL-019

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Which retained property would this named practice implement?

  2. Is the practice cheaper and more effective than a native alternative?

  3. Would adoption close a failure mode or merely improve Lean conformance?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

Rejected as a property; all named practices are optional implementations.

PROPERTY_ID

EL-038

PROPERTY_NAME

Improvement activity volume as evidence of improvement

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Suggestion counts, kaizen-event programmes, A3 completion and transformation dashboards.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Increase the number of suggestions, events, A3s, cycles or completed improvements.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Organisations need participation and follow-through, but activity is easier to count than effect.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None; the proxy creates a new failure mode.

MECHANISM

Reward counts, completion and cadence.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. Activity may be monitored diagnostically but not treated as outcome.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Track only material countermeasures and sample their benefit, burden and recurrence.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Outcome evidence, anti-gaming checks and no quota incentives.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

May reveal participation or process blockage as a secondary signal.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Trivial changes, paperwork, gaming, fatigue, hidden failures and permanent bureaucracy.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Kaizen/PDSA studies show method fidelity and sustainment vary; completed events do not establish customer/system payoff.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Rejected and replaced by EL-019, EL-020 and EL-025.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Count evidence-qualified outcomes and retired failures, not ritual volume.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A3 counts, kaizen newspapers, suggestion quotas and event calendars are not engineering properties.

CURRENT_STATUS

REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_CRITICAL

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S011

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S045

  2. S047

  3. S048

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S043

  2. S044

  3. S060

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Useful leading indicators of improvement capability remain context-specific and gameable.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-019

  2. EL-020

  3. EL-025

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Does the improvement metric measure changed outcomes or only completed activity?

  2. Can teams decline low-value events and retire ineffective controls without penalty?

  3. How is gaming, fatigue and maintenance burden detected?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

Rejected as outcome evidence.

EL-039

EL-039

historical_native_record
PROPERTY_ID

EL-039

PROPERTY_NAME

Universal supplier synchronisation, concentration or single sourcing

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Overgeneralisation of Toyota supplier/JIT coordination.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Tightly synchronise suppliers and reduce duplicated stocks/sources.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Uncoordinated supply can create inventory and quality problems.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None at universal scope; synchronisation can magnify common-cause failure.

MECHANISM

Concentrate volume, shorten schedules and reduce supplier/stock redundancy.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. Deep synchronisation is justified only where capability gains exceed concentration and disruption risk.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use clear interfaces, qualified alternates and ordinary supplier management for nonstrategic inputs.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Supplier viability; visibility; fair contracts; recovery; substitutability; geographic/technical risk analysis.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

May improve quality learning and transaction efficiency in stable, high-trust relationships.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Lock-in, supplier fragility, lower-tier single points, bargaining abuse and correlated disruption.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Post-2011 evidence and JIT risk studies show synchronisation/centralisation can raise disruption exposure.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Replaced by EL-027 joint capability plus EL-028 segmented resilience and alternatives.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Choose relationship depth and source redundancy by criticality, learning value and recovery economics.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Milk runs, supplier kanban and single-source scorecards are not general requirements.

CURRENT_STATUS

REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_POST_DISRUPTION

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S005

  2. S069

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S039

  2. S040

EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE

  1. S035

  2. S037

  3. S038

OPEN_QUESTIONS

Optimal balance between relationship-specific learning and diversification remains industry-specific.

IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY

SYSTEM_OWNER

FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE

Tight synchronisation can lower routine buffers but raise disruption correlation. Substitute EL-027/EL-028.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-027

  2. EL-028

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Does deeper synchronisation improve joint capability enough to justify concentration risk?

  2. Are alternate sources or designs truly independent and qualified?

  3. Who carries inventory, capital and recovery burden across the tiers?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

Rejected universal rule; selective integration remains valid.

POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY

  1. EL-027

  2. EL-028

QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT

  1. Does deeper synchronisation improve joint capability enough to justify concentration risk?

  2. Are alternate sources or designs truly independent and qualified?

  3. Who carries inventory, capital and recovery burden across the tiers?

DISPOSITION_NOTE

Rejected universal rule; selective integration remains valid.

Rejected universal with contingent supplier arrangements preserved

Disruption descriptions and R009 proposals motivate scrutiny; they do not prove that diversification always improves resilience or that integration is inherently harmful.

Duplicated qualification, lost scale and common-cause alternatives.

Compare concentration, alternatives and recovery by criticality; a qualified single source with adequate protection may be justified.

PROPERTY_ID

EL-040

PROPERTY_NAME

Immutable standardisation and procedure compliance

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Taylorist/managerial appropriation of standard work and audit-based Lean deployments.

LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION

MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Define one best way and require conformance.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Variation can be harmful, but frozen procedure prevents adaptation and learning.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

None as an immutable rule.

MECHANISM

Central authors prescribe and audit compliance; deviations are treated as worker failure.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

NO GENERAL TRIGGER. Safety-critical immutable constraints may exist, but even they require controlled review.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Use EL-017: a current baseline with explicit exceptions, change rights and review.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

If strict compliance is temporarily necessary: validated hazard case, scope, independent review, override/escalation and expiry.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Can reduce unsafe variation for stable, validated critical steps.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

Stale procedure, hidden workarounds, deskilling, loss of expertise, coercion and inability to respond to novel conditions.

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

Worker studies link workflow formalisation and autonomy loss to poorer outcomes; mature Lean interpretations describe standards as improvable baselines.

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

Rejected and replaced by lifecycle-controlled baseline plus human judgement and change authority.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

No immutable general standard; use risk-calibrated constraints within a versioned and reviewable baseline.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Standard-work sheets, SOP audits and conformance scores are not proof of capability.

CURRENT_STATUS

REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

HIGH_MIXED_SOCIAL

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  2. S008

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S025

  2. S026

  3. S028

  1. S020

  2. S031

OPEN_QUESTIONS

The boundary between mandatory safety constraints and expert discretion must be justified per hazard.

  2. Can experts raise exceptions and revise the rule through a controlled path?

  3. Does compliance suppress evidence of a changing context or unsafe workload?

Historical scientific account and full original public reader
Historical and current sources: the original Page narrative and complete property reader below retain the historical public Lean account. Its 40 original rows are not the corrected accepted study merely because current identities have the same count. LAW-L1 source genealogy and qualification records the exact later subject. Detailed current scientific interpretation remains withheld pending source-specific disclosure; family completeness keeps that limit and the unfinished LAW-S separate.

From Toyota-specific production history to portable engineering properties—without turning IMPLEMENTAUDIT into a Lean programme or toolkit.

Genealogy

  1. Toyoda loom defect-stop and Kiichiro Toyoda's needed-quantity/time ideas preceded the later jidoka and just-in-time system.
  2. Toyota combined flow with kanban, levelling, quality, maintenance, training, labour institutions, supplier relations, and bounded response to abnormality.
  3. Statistical quality and Training Within Industry are shared ancestries, not proof that either generated the complete Toyota Production System.
  4. Lean production became an external comparative category; Lean Thinking abstracted value, value stream, flow, pull, and perfection.
  5. Product-development, learning-system, and socio-technical interpretations exposed option, sustainment, worker, and power boundaries hidden by toolkit accounts.
  6. Lean software and software Kanban translated selected properties. Continuous Delivery and DevOps are hybrids and convergences, not simply direct Toyota descendants.

Selected sources and claim limits

SourceSupportsDoes not establish
Toyota, The Birth of JidokaA corporate historical account of the loom defect-stop antecedent.The complete later TPS doctrine or causal outcome proof.
Toyota, Origins of Just-in-Time and Supermarket MethodThe documented needed-quantity/time and replenishment lineage.Zero inventory or make-to-order everywhere.
Sugimori et al., Toyota production system and Kanban systemA participant account connecting JIT, kanban, and respect-for-human-system doctrine.Neutral worker-experience evidence or universal superiority.
Holweg, The genealogy of lean productionThe longer genealogy and diffusion beyond one invented moment.Validation of every later Lean claim.
Williams et al., Against lean productionCriticism of early universal-superiority and mass-production caricatures.That every Lean mechanism is ineffective.
Landsbergis et al., worker-health reviewWorkload, autonomy, and worker outcomes as first-order context.One universal labour-governance design.
Hopp and Spearman, To Pull or Not to PullMechanism- and context-sensitive pull/flow reasoning.A universal WIP or buffer formula.
Netland, Just-in-time for supply chains in turbulent timesSegmented resilience-aware JIT/JIC reasoning.Resolved quantitative calibration across domains.

Surviving properties

  • Inspect actual work and triangulate it with traceable system evidence.
  • Expose abnormality, then supply bounded stop, containment, escalation, recovery, and recurrence learning.
  • Prefer system flow and queue-aware capacity to maximum local utilisation.
  • Use WIP, batch, cadence, and buffers only when their live economics justify them.
  • Maintain a revisable baseline with challenge, qualification, expiry, and retirement.
  • Retain options only while uncertainty and irreversible commitment justify their carrying cost.

Rejected caricatures and ceremonies

Historical TPS, Lean production, Lean Thinking, learning-system Lean, and toolkit Lean are not interchangeable. Waste elimination is not the master objective. Zero inventory, maximum utilisation, zero slack, the smallest possible batch everywhere, and exact Toyota-tool conformance are rejected universals.

5S, value-stream maps, kanban boards, A3 sheets, exactly five whys, kaizen events, and standard-work forms may instantiate a property in context. Their presence is not evidence that the property works. Labour and trust language also cannot be converted into unsupported claims of autonomy, humane workload, or fair distribution.

Native route

IMPLEMENTAUDIT maps the surviving properties to existing owner/source, Smoke A/B, Andon, Hansei, recurrence, capacity, action-selection, and closure controls. The bounded cross-method changes are classified on Evolved-LAW; no Lean-specific runtime or certification was added.

Complete historical property reader

All 40 original public records appear here, including their full fields, contextual limits and negative forms. Original status and evidence labels describe that source freeze. The later accepted successor remains a separate object. Each record links to its immutable original source and the existing issue's historical native-owner mapping; those mappings confer no new current implementation acceptance.

EL-001 — Outcome and value definition with named beneficiary, constraints and time horizon
PROPERTY ID
EL-001
PROPERTY NAME
Outcome and value definition with named beneficiary, constraints and time horizon
HISTORICAL ORIGIN
Toyota's customer-first quality doctrine; Lean Thinking's value principle; later service/product critiques.
LINEAGE CLASSIFICATION
LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION
ORIGINAL FORM
Define value from the customer and orient production toward it.
PROBLEM IT ADDRESSED
Organisations can efficiently produce outputs that do not improve the beneficiary's outcome or that externalise cost.
FAILURE MODE IT TRIES TO PREVENT
Efficient delivery of low-value, harmful or locally beneficial work.
MECHANISM
Name the beneficiary and desired outcome; state safety, labour, resilience and externality constraints; choose a time horizon; test whether work changes the outcome.
TRIGGER OR CONTEXT
Multiple plausible outputs compete, resources are material, or local efficiency could displace customer/system value.
NON TRIGGER OR CHEAP PATH
For trivial, reversible work, record a one-sentence purpose and acceptance condition rather than create a value-stream artefact.
DEPENDENCIES OR PRECONDITIONS
Legitimate beneficiary representation; outcome observability; explicit non-negotiable constraints; authority to stop low-value work.
EXPECTED ENGINEERING PAYOFF
Better selection, fewer abandoned outputs, reduced proxy optimisation and clearer trade-offs.
KNOWN FAILURE MODES
Customer rhetoric masks managerial cost goals; one stakeholder's value harms another; short-horizon metrics; unmeasurable outcomes; retrospective rationalisation.
IMPORTANT CRITICISMS
Lean literature specifies waste more richly than value; customer language can ignore workers, suppliers, public risk and long-term resilience.
HOW THE PROPERTY EVOLVED
Moved from production value and waste categories toward multi-stakeholder outcome framing, product discovery and system constraints.
MATURE OR EVOLVED FORM
Use an explicit value hypothesis with beneficiary, outcome, constraints, evidence threshold, review date and abandonment rule.
CEREMONY VS PROPERTY
No Lean vocabulary, value workshop or value-stream map is required; the property is a decision-relevant outcome model.

  1. S062
  2. S060
  3. S061
OPEN QUESTIONS
How should value be aggregated when customers, workers, suppliers and public safety conflict?
IMPROVEMENT AUTHORITY
SYSTEM_OWNER
FLOW RESILIENCE PROFILE
NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
POSSIBLE CONFLICTING PROPERTY
  1. EL-009
  2. EL-023
  3. EL-008
QUESTIONS FOR REPOSITORY AUDIT
  1. Does the target system name the beneficiary, desired outcome, constraints and time horizon rather than equating output or waste removal with value?
  2. Can it stop or retire work when the value hypothesis fails?
  3. Are labour, safety, resilience and external costs prevented from disappearing behind a customer-value claim?
DISPOSITION NOTE

Immutable historical source: original ledger, /EVOLVED_LEAN_PROPERTY_LEDGER/0, SHA-256 5d82d354bbd0dc0bfbc2dd2020f15556dfc5fee964a5d61e68be06abe5eb6d3a.

Existing issue mapping: R0025/#186; native owners R001D, R001F, R0022; historical native disposition PC. Preserve named consumer, outcome and time horizon when consequential; no value-stream ceremony for small owner-supplied tasks. This is the existing historical mapping, not a requalification of current native behavior.

EL-002 — End-to-end system boundary and flow/queue/handoff visibility
PROPERTY ID
EL-002
PROPERTY NAME
End-to-end system boundary and flow/queue/handoff visibility
HISTORICAL ORIGIN
TPS material/information flow; Lean value-stream generalisation; later service/software translations.
LINEAGE CLASSIFICATION
LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION
ORIGINAL FORM
See the whole path from demand through production and delivery rather than optimise isolated machines or departments.
PROBLEM IT ADDRESSED
Local gains create downstream queues, rework, handoff loss or delayed customer outcomes.
FAILURE MODE IT TRIES TO PREVENT
Local optimisation and invisible waiting across organisational boundaries.
MECHANISM
Define the relevant path and work classes; observe arrivals, queues, processing, rework, handoffs, dependencies and outcome latency; assign end-to-end ownership.
TRIGGER OR CONTEXT
Repeated handoffs, unexplained elapsed time, departmental optimisation or customer delay dominate processing time.
NON TRIGGER OR CHEAP PATH
For a short single-owner path, a simple trace or sampled timeline is enough; no wall-sized map.
DEPENDENCIES OR PRECONDITIONS
Stable item identity; traceability across boundaries; truthful timestamps/states; an owner able to resolve cross-boundary causes.
EXPECTED ENGINEERING PAYOFF
Reduced total lead time, fewer handoff failures, better bottleneck and queue decisions.
KNOWN FAILURE MODES
Map theatre; stale diagrams; excessive instrumentation; forced linear path for exploratory work; omission of externalities; local teams unable to change system constraints.
IMPORTANT CRITICISMS
Value-stream maps are frequently local snapshots and can mistake visible factory-like sequence for the real distributed causal system.
HOW THE PROPERTY EVOLVED
Value-stream mapping was generalised beyond factories; modern software combines traces and delivery telemetry, while discovery work may require networks rather than linear streams.
MATURE OR EVOLVED FORM
Maintain the cheapest decision-useful end-to-end model, including feedback/rework loops, queues, dependencies and outcome—not just touch time.
CEREMONY VS PROPERTY
A value-stream map is one implementation. Event logs, service blueprints, dependency graphs or sampled traces may be stronger.

EVIDENCE STRENGTH
HIGH_CONVERGENT

OPEN QUESTIONS
How should boundaries be chosen in multi-product, platform or ecosystem settings without creating an unmanageable total map?
IMPROVEMENT AUTHORITY
CROSS_FUNCTIONAL
FLOW RESILIENCE PROFILE
End-to-end latency, queue and handoff profile; assumes valid item identity and boundary. Over-instrumentation and forced linearisation are failure modes.
POSSIBLE CONFLICTING PROPERTY
  1. EL-018
  2. EL-029
QUESTIONS FOR REPOSITORY AUDIT
  1. Does the system expose end-to-end waiting, queues, handoffs, rework and outcome latency rather than only local activity?
  2. Is the map or trace current enough to change a decision?
  3. Does it preserve a cheap path where the work is already short, observable and single-owner?

EL-003 — Workplace and work-state legibility
PROPERTY ID
EL-003
PROPERTY NAME
Workplace and work-state legibility
HISTORICAL ORIGIN
TPS visual control/5S/andon; later service and software visualisation.
LINEAGE CLASSIFICATION
DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
ORIGINAL FORM
Arrange physical work so expected state, ownership, missing items and abnormalities are easy to discern.
PROBLEM IT ADDRESSED
Search, ambiguity and hidden state delay action or conceal abnormal work.
FAILURE MODE IT TRIES TO PREVENT
Undetected abnormality, lost work, excessive search and ambiguous ownership.
MECHANISM
Expose state and limits at the point of work using labels, layout, telemetry, state models or visual cues proportional to decision need.
TRIGGER OR CONTEXT
Work is repeatedly lost, searched for, misrouted, duplicated or left in an ambiguous state.
NON TRIGGER OR CHEAP PATH
When state is already obvious and low-risk, use naming conventions or a small status field; do not create ritual boards or audit scoring.
DEPENDENCIES OR PRECONDITIONS
Shared state definitions; maintenance ownership; accessibility; protection against surveillance and stale displays.
EXPECTED ENGINEERING PAYOFF
Faster recognition, lower search/coordination cost, earlier abnormality detection.
KNOWN FAILURE MODES
5S audit theatre; cosmetic tidiness; information overload; stale boards; worker surveillance; local legibility hiding end-to-end state.
IMPORTANT CRITICISMS
Evidence for exact 5S bundles is small and heterogeneous; legibility can become discipline and scoring rather than aid.
HOW THE PROPERTY EVOLVED
Expanded from physical workplace order to digital work-state and observability; mature form separates human legibility from metric control.
MATURE OR EVOLVED FORM
Make the minimum relevant normal/abnormal state visible to people who can act, with freshness, accessibility and ownership controls.
CEREMONY VS PROPERTY
5S labels, shadow boards and kanban columns are optional; cleanliness scores are not the property.

  1. Can responsible people tell the current and abnormal state without reconstructing it manually?
  2. Are displays fresh, action-linked and non-punitive rather than ceremonial surveillance?
  3. Would additional visual management solve a real ambiguity or merely add another board?

EL-004 — Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits
PROPERTY ID
EL-004
PROPERTY NAME
Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits
HISTORICAL ORIGIN
Toyota supermarket/kanban; operations-science formalisation; software Kanban translation.
LINEAGE CLASSIFICATION
DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
ORIGINAL FORM
Downstream withdrawal and explicit limits prevent premature production and excess work in process.
PROBLEM IT ADDRESSED
Unbounded release creates congestion, long feedback, priority churn and hidden defects.
FAILURE MODE IT TRIES TO PREVENT
Queue growth, overproduction, premature commitment and excessive context switching.
MECHANISM
Set an explicit bound or release rule for active commitments; admit new work when capacity becomes available; define exceptions and service classes.
TRIGGER OR CONTEXT
Arrival pressure exceeds completion capacity, active work accumulates, or long WIP correlates with delay/quality loss.
NON TRIGGER OR CHEAP PATH
For low-volume work with naturally bounded commitments, use a simple finish-before-start norm or periodic capacity check.
DEPENDENCIES OR PRECONDITIONS
Observable WIP boundary; stable item definitions; capacity/replenishment capability; priority and exception policy; no hidden pre-entry queue.
EXPECTED ENGINEERING PAYOFF
Shorter and more predictable lead time, clearer constraints, faster defect feedback.
KNOWN FAILURE MODES
Starvation; hidden backlog before entry; arbitrary limits; gaming item size/status; blocking critical long-lead work; apparent cycle-time gains through exclusion.
IMPORTANT CRITICISMS
A pull signal is not trustworthy demand or capacity; board-level limits can displace queues and invite metric gaming.
HOW THE PROPERTY EVOLVED
Pull was clarified as WIP limitation rather than cards or make-to-order; software translation retained limits but evidence shows optimal limits are contextual.
MATURE OR EVOLVED FORM
Use empirical limits by work class, monitor blocked/aged work and total demand, and adapt limits with capacity, variability and service risk.
CEREMONY VS PROPERTY
Kanban cards/boards are not required. A database rule, token, CONWIP, admission policy or finite-capacity scheduler can implement it.
Status at this source freeze
RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM
EVIDENCE STRENGTH
HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL
PRIMARY SOURCES
  1. S003
  2. S005
  3. S006
CRITICAL SOURCES

EL-005 — Batch-size economics and small reversible increments
PROPERTY ID
EL-005
PROPERTY NAME
Batch-size economics and small reversible increments
HISTORICAL ORIGIN
TPS setup reduction and small lots; Lean product development and Continuous Delivery translation.
LINEAGE CLASSIFICATION
HYBRID
ORIGINAL FORM
Reduce lot size to shorten feedback and lower inventory once setup costs permit.
PROBLEM IT ADDRESSED
Large batches delay information, amplify rework and make failures expensive.
FAILURE MODE IT TRIES TO PREVENT
Late defect discovery, large release risk, long queue time and irreversible commitment.
MECHANISM
Model batch benefits against setup, validation, coordination and transport costs; reduce setup first; choose a reversible economic batch.
TRIGGER OR CONTEXT
Feedback delay or failure blast radius is material and switching/release cost can be reduced.
NON TRIGGER OR CHEAP PATH
If changes are already tiny or validation has a fixed high cost, keep the economic batch and improve feedback within it.
DEPENDENCIES OR PRECONDITIONS
Measurable setup/release costs; modular work; validation capability; rollback/recovery; ability to integrate increments.
EXPECTED ENGINEERING PAYOFF
Faster learning, smaller failures, lower inventory and release risk.
KNOWN FAILURE MODES
One-piece-flow dogma; fragmentation overhead; excessive coordination; local micro-batches accumulating into system batches; unsafe partial delivery.
IMPORTANT CRITICISMS
Smallness is not value; a minimum batch can be uneconomic, and knowledge work may require coherent experimental units.
HOW THE PROPERTY EVOLVED
Generalised from physical lots to information, decisions and software changes; product development added option preservation rather than indiscriminate decomposition.
MATURE OR EVOLVED FORM
Select batch size from total economics and reversibility, not a universal minimum; make delayed feedback and blast radius explicit.
CEREMONY VS PROPERTY
One-piece flow, daily releases or story slicing are implementations, not the property.

EL-006 — Setup, changeover, release and feedback latency reduction
PROPERTY ID
EL-006
PROPERTY NAME
Setup, changeover, release and feedback latency reduction
HISTORICAL ORIGIN
Toyota setup-time reduction and industrial engineering; later software deployment automation.
LINEAGE CLASSIFICATION
DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
ORIGINAL FORM
Lower die-change and process-switching time so mixed production and small lots become economical.
PROBLEM IT ADDRESSED
High transition cost forces large batches, inflexibility and infrequent feedback.
FAILURE MODE IT TRIES TO PREVENT
Long feedback loops, large economic batches, downtime and costly recovery.
MECHANISM
Separate necessary from avoidable setup; externalise safe preparation; simplify interfaces; automate repeatable checks; measure total transition and recovery.
TRIGGER OR CONTEXT
Setup, integration, release or environment preparation materially determines batch size, delay or downtime.
NON TRIGGER OR CHEAP PATH
If setup is rare and immaterial, document the path and avoid an optimisation programme.
DEPENDENCIES OR PRECONDITIONS
Stable interfaces; safe automation; competent maintainers; configuration control; rollback and qualification.
EXPECTED ENGINEERING PAYOFF
Greater flexibility, smaller economic batches, higher availability and faster learning.
KNOWN FAILURE MODES
Unsafe shortcuts; preparation shifted to another team; brittle automation; ergonomic or quality risk; faster release of low-value work.
IMPORTANT CRITICISMS
Local setup savings often omit preparation, validation and recovery transferred elsewhere.
HOW THE PROPERTY EVOLVED
Extended from physical die changes to software pipelines, environment provisioning, integration and decision-feedback latency.
MATURE OR EVOLVED FORM
Reduce total safe transition cost, including preparation, validation, recovery and burden transferred across boundaries.
CEREMONY VS PROPERTY
SMED workshops, coloured steps and checklists are optional; lower total transition cost is the property.

EL-007 — Demand-capacity pacing and production levelling
PROPERTY ID
EL-007
PROPERTY NAME
Demand-capacity pacing and production levelling
HISTORICAL ORIGIN
Kiichiro/Ohno JIT, takt and heijunka in repetitive automotive production.
LINEAGE CLASSIFICATION
DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
ORIGINAL FORM
Use demand pace and mix levelling to coordinate repetitive production and reduce unevenness.
PROBLEM IT ADDRESSED
Uncoordinated release creates surges, starvation, overtime and supplier amplification.
FAILURE MODE IT TRIES TO PREVENT
Demand/capacity mismatch, violent mix changes, uneven workloads and hidden overburden.
MECHANISM
Estimate sustainable pace by work class; level the predictable component; explicitly buffer residual variation; replan when assumptions break.
TRIGGER OR CONTEXT
Repeatable work, measurable aggregate demand, flexible capacity and feasible mix sequencing.
NON TRIGGER OR CHEAP PATH
For unique, exploratory or very low-volume work, use finite-capacity planning, rolling forecasts and explicit priority rather than takt ceremony.
DEPENDENCIES OR PRECONDITIONS
Demand information; cross-trained/flexible resources; setup capability; exception policy; workload and ergonomic safeguards; buffers.
EXPECTED ENGINEERING PAYOFF
Smoother flow, less oscillation, clearer overload and more stable supplier/capacity planning.
KNOWN FAILURE MODES
False precision; backlog or overtime used to hide variation; forced pace and work intensification; brittle high-mix schedules; supplier burden transfer.
IMPORTANT CRITICISMS
Takt can become a coercive pace standard; conventional levelling has weak applicability in high-mix/low-volume or discovery work.
HOW THE PROPERTY EVOLVED
Later research narrows conventional levelling to stable/repetitive contexts and develops HMLV adaptations; resilience hybrids preserve reserves.
MATURE OR EVOLVED FORM
Level only forecastable aggregate demand, maintain capacity/time buffers and protect workload limits; treat takt as diagnostic, not a metronome.
CEREMONY VS PROPERTY
Takt boards, heijunka boxes and fixed pitch are manufacturing implementations, not general requirements.
